{
  "raw": "Registry 更新完成。结果解读：\n\n---\n\n## α=0.65 s42 fp32 分析结果\n\n### 核心指标（D105+D111 校正后）\n\n| 指标 | α=0.50 | α=0.55 | α=0.60 | **α=0.65** | α=0.75 |\n|------|--------|--------|--------|------------|--------|\n| PermG_fwd | 6/8 | 0/8 | 0/8 | **0/8** | 6/8 |\n| K≥3(3σ) | 6 | 1 | 0 | **1** | 6 |\n| K≥4(3σ) | 3 | 0 | 0 | **0** | 0 |\n| threshold_onset_3σ | - | - | - | **0/8** | - |\n| diff_xcorr | - | - | - | **6/8** | - |\n\n### 结论\n\n1. **PermG_fwd = 0/8** — α=0.65 仍完全被压制，与 α=0.60 相同。Sync collapse dip 扩展至 **[0.55, 0.65]**，不限于 [0.55, 0.60]。\n\n2. **恢复是突变而非渐进** — 从 α=0.65 (0/8) 到 α=0.75 (6/8) 是跳变。PermG 在 dip 区间内没有中间恢复值（0/8→0/8→0/8→6/8）。转折点在 (0.65, 0.75) 区间内。\n\n3. **PermG 边缘效应** — ece→distinct 三对 p_raw=0.014–0.025（名义显著），但 FDR 校正后 p_fdr=0.067（不显著）。比 α=0.60 更接近恢复临界值，提示 α=0.70 可能是实际转折点。\n\n4. **Drift 对比** — perplexity drift +123.75%（介于 α=0.60 和 α=0.75 之间），distinct 退化约 -30%~-37%，mauve 仅 -5.23%（显著弱于其他 α）。Downstream 退化幅度不足以触发 3σ 阈值，这解释了 threshold_onset_3sigma=0/8。\n\n5. **D105 关键发现** — canary 在 gen1-2 就 onset，但 downstream 指标从未越过 3σ 门槛。退化存在但不够剧烈——canary-downstream 在 temporal scale 上同步（sync collapse），剥夺了 lead-lag 信号。"
}

This is a very important result for the sync dip mapping. Let me verify the analysis independently by checking the server.

Key findings:
1. **PermG_fwd = 0/8 at α=0.65**: The sync dip extends to [0.55, 0.65], not just [0.55, 0.60]
2. **Sharp transition**: 0/8 at α=0.65 → 6/8 at α=0.75 (jump, not gradual)
3. **Near-miss FDR**: ece→distinct pairs have raw p=0.014-0.025 but FDR p=0.067 — close but not significant


4. **Threshold_onset_3sigma = 0/8**: Downstream drift exists but doesn't cross the 3σ threshold

This aligns with the S×R competition model—at α=0.65, the signal S is strengthening while temporal resolution R is degrading, causing both canary and downstream to collapse together and keeping PermG at 0/8. Now I need to check the 5method_summary on the server to verify these patterns.

This is a process issue — the analysis script needs to be updated to use the new naming convention.

Looking at the D111 direction check note, it shows the direction filter was applied and reduced K3 from 6 to 1 and K4 from 1 to 0, but there's no mention of TY direction filtering. The ambiguity here is whether ty_granger represents forward-only or total counts, and since the old field was reverse-direction before the fix, I need to verify if this value has the correct direction applied — though C003 already flags TY as unreliable at low α anyway.

The bigger issue is that the worker output shows a registry update with PermG_fwd=0/8 and drift metrics, but there's no analysis/ directory on the server with the standard 5method_summary.txt, which means the analysis results weren't generated by the server-side script and I can't verify reproducibility.
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am operating as an independent scientific reviewer (Reviewer role) for the `distrib_canary_collapse` ML research project targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR June cycle. My ongoing responsibilities are: (a) audit experiment conclusions against server-side ground truth, (b) verify data integrity across registry/server/CSV, (c) review worker outputs for correctness and methodological soundness, (d) flag issues to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. All output must be in Chinese. In this session specifically, I:
   - Performed an initial GPU/experiment state audit (4 RUNNING experiments on westd-17855)
   - Reviewed 18+ worker completions and experiment events
   - Identified and reported registry inconsistencies, methodological gaps, and process regressions
   - Verified CSV bug fixes, registry field cleanups, and new experiment results

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **PermG (Permutation Granger)**: Primary canary detection method. FDR-corrected across 8 canonical pairs {te,ece}×{d1,d2,d3,mauve}. Zero-FPR in null (0/24). Has FPR inflation under non-stationarity (φ≥0.95 → ~10.7%)
   - **K≥3 Consensus**: Number of metric pairs where ≥3/5 methods agree on forward causal direction. Equal-weight, with D111 direction filter (peak_lag < 0)
   - **Sync Dip / S×R Competition**: α∈[0.55, 0.65] shows PermG=0/8 despite large drift — canary and downstream collapse simultaneously, destroying lead-lag signal. Confirmed extended to [0.55, 0.65] (was thought to be only α=0.60)
   - **D111 Direction Filter**: Forward causation requires peak_lag < 0. Was missing from CSV compilation script (now fixed)
   - **D105 3σ Threshold**: True 3-sigma threshold for threshold_onset method, with FDR correction
   - **Sigmoid Phase Transition**: PermG detection rate follows step-like function at α_c ≈ 0.48 (T=10). Steepness parameter k unconstrained (SE ≈ k)
   - **Power Theory √T scaling**: Predicts α_c left-shift with increasing T. T=20 α=0.45 discriminating test: predicted 0/8, actual 5/8
   - **First-Differencing vs PermG**: J2 rebuttal — differencing loses 39% power at T=11 but maintains size control. PermG retains 80% power but has mild FPR inflation under non-stationarity
   - **Empirical AR(1) coefficients**: distinct_1 raw φ=0.86 (from deterministic trend), but first-differenced φ=0.17 (near white noise). Supports PermG safety for actual data
   - **Reactive Intervention**: α=1.0 with distinct_1 monitoring, trigger at gen 2 → switch to α=0.0 → recovery by gen 3-4. Cross-seed consistent (s42, s43)
   - **Registry field standardization**: Old fields (ty_granger, K_ge3, consensus_k3_3sigma) → new fields (ty_forward_sig, robust_pairs_K3)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`artifacts/sigmoid_fit_j4_results.json`** (local workspace)
     - J4 rebuttal sigmoid steepness analysis
     - T=10: k=478±475, α_c=0.483; Combined: k=279±528, α_c=0.451
     - All models (sigmoid/probit/gen_logistic) indistinguishable, data is step function
   
   - **`artifacts/j2_first_diff_power_simulation.json`** (local workspace)
     - First simulation: stationary DGP (φ=0.5), T=11, 1000 reps
     - Diff loses 35-45% power, PermG retains 75-85%
   
   - **`artifacts/j2_nonstationary_size_power.json`** (local workspace)
     - Second simulation: φ∈{0.5, 0.95, 0.99}, size + power
     - KEY: PermG FPR at φ=0.99 = 10.7%, First-diff FPR = 4.2%
     - JSON conclusion string erroneously says PermG FPR "near 5%"
   
   - **`artifacts/j2_nonstationary_sim.py`** (local workspace)
     - Simulation code: bivariate AR(1) DGP, F-test via numpy OLS, permutation with rng.permutation(x)
     - Code verified correct
   
   - **`artifacts/empirical_ar1_coefficients.json`** (local workspace)
     - 7 experiments, raw + differenced AR(1) for token_entropy and distinct_1
     - d1 raw φ mean=0.89 (trend), d1 diff φ mean=0.17 (near white noise)
   
   - **`artifacts/dose_response_d120_recompiled.csv`** (local workspace)
     - CSV compilation with D111 direction filter fix applied
     - T=20 values verified: a045 K3=3, a060 K3=4, a075 K3=6 (all match server)
     - 71 rows total, 33 rows with K3 changes from bug fix
   
   - **`registry.yaml`** (local workspace)
     - Multiple cleanup rounds: OLD_* fields (6 experiments), then top-level old fields (25 experiments, 45 fields), then variant fields (26 experiments, 30 fields)
     - a060_s43 critical conflict resolved: consensus_k3_3sigma=7/8 deleted (robust_pairs_K3=0 is correct)
     - Nested lead_lag sub-dict fields remain (low risk)
     - NEW regression: a065_s42_fp32 entry uses old field names
   
   - **Server: westd-17855 `/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/results/`**
     - `alpha_sweep/a065_s42_fp32/analysis/5method_summary.txt`: PermG=0/8, K≥3=1/8, TY=4/8
     - `intervention/reactive_d1_s43_fp32/`: gen_0-gen_10 complete, trigger_gen=2, recovery by gen 3-4
     - `scale_check/pythia69b_wiki_a100_s43_fp32/`: OOM failure, gen_0+gen_1 only
     - Disk: 350G total, ~297G used, ~54G free (85%)

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Registry exp_id filter returns empty**: `{"exp_id": "alpha_sweep_a075_s42"}` returns 0 results via MCP tool. Fix: used Grep on registry.yaml directly, or used limit-based queries.
   - **interv_react_s43 stale data**: Old killed run left gen_2-gen_10 directories. Fix: worker_ops_interv_stale_data_cleanup deleted old gen directories.
   - **Registry conclusion="killed" on active processes**: Two restarted experiments kept old conclusion. Fix: worker_ops_clear_stale_conclusion removed conclusion field.
   - **CSV consensus_ge3 bug**: compile_dose_response.py missing D111 direction filter. Fix: worker_ops_r5_csv_k3_bugfix added peak_lag<0 check. 33/71 rows changed.
   - **Registry old/new field conflicts**: consensus_k3_3sigma=7/8 vs robust_pairs_K3=0. Fix: Two rounds of cleanup removed 75+ old field instances across 51 experiments.
   - **generalize_pythia1b_c4_a100_s43_v2 HF Hub failure**: Missing env vars (HF_HOME, TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE). Fix: worker used local model path and proper env setup.
   - **scale_69b_a100_s43_fp32 OOM**: Pythia-6.9B fp32 exceeds 96GB GPU. Previous runs used bf16. Recommended bf16.
   - **a065 analysis worker field naming regression**: New entry uses old field names after cleanup. Reported but not yet fixed.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **J2 First-Differencing rebuttal**: Built complete evidence chain across 3 analyses: (1) stationary sim showing diff power loss, (2) non-stationary sim showing PermG FPR inflation, (3) empirical AR(1) showing actual data φ_diff << 0.95. Recommended dual-evidence strategy: theoretical (φ<0.95) + empirical (0/24 FPR).
   - **Sync dip boundary mapping**: Confirmed dip extends to [0.55, 0.65] with α=0.65 result (PermG=0/8). Transition to detection (6/8) is sharp, occurs in (0.65, 0.75). Recommended α=0.70 as next data point.
   - **J4 Sigmoid steepness**: Identified that k parameter is unconstrained — cannot characterize transition shape, only confirm "sharp/step-like". Combined fit methodology flawed (pooling different T curves).
   - **19828 GPU utilization**: Flagged idle GPUs, subsequently utilized for resubmitted experiments.
   - **Disk space monitoring**: Flagged 54GB free on 17855 with ~44GB concurrent demand. scale_69b OOM resolved the largest consumer.

6. All User Messages:
   - "Continue the conversation from where it left off without asking the user any further questions. Resume directly..." (initial resume)
   - "Tool loaded." (multiple times — after ToolSearch operations)

7. Pending Tasks:
   - **a065 registry field normalization**: New entry uses old field names (ty_granger, K3_3sigma etc.) — needs correction to standard format (ty_forward_sig, robust_pairs_K3)

token_entropy phi ranges [-0.51, -0.10] — anti-correlated or near zero, far below 0.95. (2) At a=0.75-1.00, token_entropy phi reaches 0.14-1.00, but this reflects the deterministic collapse trend (the signal itself), not stochastic persistence. For distinct_1, raw phi is uniformly high (0.81-0.97) across all alpha including null, driven by a smooth monotonic trend inherent to the iterative training process. Critically, first-differenced distinct_1 phi averages only 0.17 (range [-0.14, 0.44]), confirming the increments are near-white-noise. First-differenced token_entropy under null/low-alpha averages -0.24, also far from 0.95. The high raw phi in distinct_1 and in strong-signal token_entropy is deterministic trend, not the stochastic autocorrelation that inflates PermG FPR. PermG size control is safe for our data because the stochastic component (first differences) has phi well below 0.95 across all experiments.\",\n  \"note\": \"AR(1) estimates from very short series (T=10: n=11 points, T=20: n=21 points) have large sampling variance. Interpret individual values cautiously; the pattern across experiments is more informative than any single estimate.\"\n}\n",

{
  "parentUuid": "45d00793-b0d0-4483-942b-cedc1bab7f8d",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/j2_nonstationary_sim.py",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/j2_nonstationary_sim.py",
        "content": "\"\"\"\nJ2 Non-Stationary DGP: Size + Power Simulation\nBivariate AR(1) with phi in {0.50, 0.95, 0.99}, T=11\nMethods: Raw Granger, First-Diff Granger, Permutation Granger\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport warnings\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy import stats as sp_stats\nfrom tqdm import tqdm\n\nwarnings.filterwarnings(\"ignore\")\n\nSEED = 42\nT = 11\nBURN_IN = 50\nALPHA = 0.05\nMAX_LAG = 1\nPHIS = [0.50, 0.95, 0.99]\nBETAS_SIZE = [0.0]\nBETAS_POWER = [0.1, 0.3, 0.6]\nN_PERMS = 500\n\n\ndef generate_bivariate_ar1(phi, beta, T, burn_in, rng):\n    total = T + burn_in\n    x = np.zeros(total)\n    y = np.zeros(total)\n    eps_x = rng.standard_normal(total)\n    eps_y = rng.standard_normal(total)\n    for t in range(1, total):\n        x[t] = phi * x[t-1] + eps_x[t]\n        y[t] = phi * y[t-1] + beta * x[t-1] + eps_y[t]\n    return x[burn_in:], y[burn_in:]\n\n\ndef granger_f_stat(x, y, max_lag=1):\n    \"\"\"Compute Granger F-stat: does x Granger-cause y? Returns (F, p-value).\n    Uses direct OLS — much faster than statsmodels VAR for the permutation loop.\n    \"\"\"\n    n = len(x)\n    # Build lagged data: y[lag:] ~ const + y[lag-1:n-1] + x[lag-1:n-1]\n    Y = y[max_lag:]\n    n_obs = len(Y)\n\n    # Restricted: Y ~ const + Y_lag\n    X_r = np.column_stack([np.ones(n_obs), y[max_lag-1:n-1]])\n    # Unrestricted: Y ~ const + Y_lag + X_lag\n    X_u = np.column_stack([X_r, x[max_lag-1:n-1]])\n\n    # OLS via lstsq\n    _, rss_r, _, _ = np.linalg.lstsq(X_r, Y, rcond=None)\n    _, rss_u, _, _ = np.linalg.lstsq(X_u, Y, rcond=None)\n\n    # Handle edge case: lstsq may return empty rss for rank-deficient\n    if len(rss_r) == 0:\n        rss_r = np.sum((Y - X_r @ np.linalg.lstsq(X_r, Y, rcond=None)[0])**2)\n    else:\n        rss_r = rss_r[0]\n    if len(rss_u) == 0:\n        rss_u = np.sum((Y - X_u @ np.linalg.lstsq(X_u, Y, rcond=None)[0])**2)\n    else:\n        rss_u = rss_u[0]\n\n    q = max_lag  # number of restrictions\n    k_u = X_u.shape[1]  # params in unrestricted\n    df_denom = n_obs - k_u\n\n    if df_denom <= 0 or rss_u <= 0:\n        return 0.0, 1.0\n\n    F = ((rss_r - rss_u) / q) / (rss_u / df_denom)\n    p = 1.0 - sp_stats.f.cdf(F, q, df_denom)\n    return F, p\n\n\ndef raw_granger_pvalue(x, y, max_lag=1):\n    _, p = granger_f_stat(x, y, max_lag)\n    return p\n\n\ndef diff_granger_pvalue(x, y, max_lag=1):\n    dx = np.diff(x)\n    dy = np.diff(y)\n    _, p = granger_f_stat(dx, dy, max_lag)\n    return p\n\n\ndef permutation_granger(x, y, n_perms, max_lag, rng):\n    obs_F, _ = granger_f_stat(x, y, max_lag)\n\n    count = 0\n    for _ in range(n_perms):\n        x_perm = rng.permutation(x)\n        perm_F, _ = granger_f_stat(x_perm, y, max_lag)\n        if perm_F >= obs_F:\n            count += 1\n\n    return (count + 1) / (n_perms + 1)\n\n\ndef run_simulation(n_reps, n_perms, phis, betas, seed, label=\"\"):\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    results = {}\n\n    total_conditions = len(phis) * len(betas)\n    condition_idx = 0\n\n    for phi in phis:\n        results[str(phi)] = {}\n        for beta in betas:\n            condition_idx += 1\n            desc = f\"{label} phi={phi}, beta={beta} [{condition_idx}/{total_conditions}]\"\n\n            raw_reject = 0\n            diff_reject = 0\n            perm_reject = 0\n            raw_fail = 0\n            diff_fail = 0\n            perm_fail = 0\n\n            for _ in tqdm(range(n_reps), desc=desc, leave=True):\n                x, y = generate_bivariate_ar1(phi, beta, T, BURN_IN, rng)\n\n                try:\n                    p = raw_granger_pvalue(x, y, MAX_LAG)\n                    if p < ALPHA:\n                        raw_reject += 1\n                except Exception:\n                    raw_fail += 1\n\n                try:\n                    p = diff_granger_pvalue(x, y, MAX_LAG)\n                    if p < ALPHA:\n                        diff_reject += 1\n                except Exception:\n                    diff_fail += 1\n\n                try:\n                    p = permutation_granger(x, y, n_perms, MAX_LAG, rng)\n                    if p < ALPHA:\n                        perm_reject += 1\n                except Exception:\n                    perm_fail += 1\n\n            valid_raw = n_reps - raw_fail\n            valid_diff = n_reps - diff_fail\n            valid_perm = n_reps - perm_fail\n\n            results[str(phi)][str(beta)] = {\n                \"raw_rate\": round(raw_reject / valid_raw, 4) if valid_raw > 0 else None,\n                \"diff_rate\": round(diff_reject / valid_diff, 4) if valid_diff > 0 else None,\n                \"perm_rate\": round(perm_reject / valid_perm, 4) if valid_perm > 0 else None,\n                \"raw_fail\": raw_fail,\n                \"diff_fail\": diff_fail,\n                \"perm_fail\": perm_fail,\n                \"n_reps\": n_reps,\n            }\n            print(f\"  -> Raw={results[str(phi)][str(beta)]['raw_rate']}, \"\n                  f\"Diff={results[str(phi)][str(beta)]['diff_rate']}, \"\n                  f\"Perm={results[str(phi)][str(beta)]['perm_rate']}\")\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef main():\n    dry_run = \"--dry-run\" in sys.argv\n    n_reps = 10 if dry_run else 1000\n    n_perms = 50 if dry_run else N_PERMS\n\n    print(f\"{'DRY RUN (10 reps, 50 perms)' if dry_run else 'FULL RUN (1000 reps, 500 perms)'}\")\n    print(f\"T={T}, burn_in={BURN_IN}, alpha={ALPHA}, seed={SEED}\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n\n    print(\"\\n=== Part A: Size Simulation (H0: beta=0) ===\")\n    size_results = run_simulation(n_reps, n_perms, PHIS, BETAS_SIZE, SEED, label=\"Size\")\n\n    print(\"\\n=== Part B: Power Simulation (H1: beta>0) ===\")\n    power_results = run_simulation(n_reps, n_perms, PHIS, BETAS_POWER, SEED + 1, label=\"Power\")\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - t0\n    print(f\"\\nTotal time: {elapsed/60:.1f} minutes\")\n\n    size_formatted = {}\n    for phi in PHIS:\n        d = size_results[str(phi)][\"0.0\"]\n        size_formatted[str(phi)] = {\n            \"raw_fpr\": d[\"raw_rate\"],\n            \"diff_fpr\": d[\"diff_rate\"],\n            \"perm_fpr\": d[\"perm_rate\"],\n            \"raw_fail_count\": d[\"raw_fail\"],\n            \"diff_fail_count\": d[\"diff_fail\"],\n            \"perm_fail_count\": d[\"perm_fail\"],\n        }\n\n    power_formatted = {}\n    for phi in PHIS:\n        power_formatted[str(phi)] = {}\n        for beta in BETAS_POWER:\n            d = power_results[str(phi)][str(beta)]\n            power_formatted[str(phi)][str(beta)] = {\n                \"raw_power\": d[\"raw_rate\"],\n                \"diff_power\": d[\"diff_rate\"],\n                \"perm_power\": d[\"perm_rate\"],\n                \"raw_fail_count\": d[\"raw_fail\"],\n                \"diff_fail_count\": d[\"diff_fail\"],\n                \"perm_fail_count\": d[\"perm_fail\"],\n            }\n\n    raw_95 = size_formatted[\"0.95\"][\"raw_fpr\"]\n    raw_99 = size_formatted[\"0.99\"][\"raw_fpr\"]\n    diff_95 = size_formatted[\"0.95\"][\"diff_fpr\"]\n    diff_99 = size_formatted[\"0.99\"][\"diff_fpr\"]\n    perm_95 = size_formatted[\"0.95\"][\"perm_fpr\"]\n    perm_99 = size_formatted[\"0.99\"][\"perm_fpr\"]\n\n    conclusion = (\n        f\"Size control: Raw Granger FPR inflates to {raw_95} (phi=0.95) and {raw_99} (phi=0.99), \"\n        f\"confirming spurious rejection under non-stationarity. \"\n        f\"First-diff Granger maintains FPR at {diff_95}/{diff_99}, \"\n        f\"and Permutation Granger at {perm_95}/{perm_99}, \"\n        f\"both near nominal 5%. \"\n        f\"Power: See power_results for beta-specific detection rates across phi values.\"\n    )\n\n    output = {\n        \"dgp\": {\n            \"T\": T,\n            \"burn_in\": BURN_IN,\n            \"n_reps\": n_reps,\n            \"n_perms\": n_perms,\n            \"phis\": PHIS,\n            \"betas_power\": BETAS_POWER,\n            \"seed\": SEED,\n            \"alpha\": ALPHA,\n        },\n        \"size_results\": size_formatted,\n        \"power_results\": power_formatted,\n        \"conclusion\": conclusion,\n        \"elapsed_minutes\": round(elapsed / 60, 1),\n    }\n\n    out_path = \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/j2_nonstationary_size_power.json\"\n    with open(out_path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(output, f, indent=2)\n    print(f\"\\nSaved to {out_path}\")\n\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"SUMMARY: Size (FPR at alpha=0.05)\")\n    print(f\"{'phi':<8} {'Raw':>10} {'Diff':>10} {'Perm':>10}\")\n    print(\"-\" * 40)\n    for phi in PHIS:\n        d = size_formatted[str(phi)]\n        print(f\"{phi:<8} {d['raw_fpr']:>10} {d['diff_fpr']:>10} {d['perm_fpr']:>10}\")\n\n    print(\"\\nSUMMARY: Power (rejection rate at alpha=0.05)\")\n    print(f\"{'phi':<8} {'beta':<8} {'Raw':>10} {'Diff':>10} {'Perm':>10}\")\n    print(\"-\" * 48)\n    for phi in PHIS:\n        for beta in BETAS_POWER:\n            d = power_formatted[str(phi)][str(beta)]\n            print(f\"{phi:<8} {beta:<8} {d['raw_power']:>10} {d['diff_power']:>10} {d['perm_power']:>10}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n",

 },\n  \"conclusion\": \"Size control: Raw Granger FPR inflates from 6.4% (phi=0.5) to 10.0% (phi=0.95) and 11.2% (phi=0.99), confirming spurious rejection under near-unit-root non-stationarity. First-differenced Granger maintains well-calibrated FPR (6.6%/5.9%/4.2%). Permutation Granger also shows mild inflation (6.8%/9.5%/10.7%), indicating it does not fully correct for non-stationarity in raw levels. Power: At T=11, all methods have low power for small effects (beta=0.1: 5-12%). For large effects (beta=0.6), Raw Granger shows highest nominal power (38-46%) but this is inflated by size distortion. First-diff Granger has the lowest power (18-29%) but is the only method with proper size control. Permutation Granger achieves intermediate power (37-42%) with mildly inflated size.\",\n  \"elapsed_minutes\": 33.4,\n  \"implementation_note\": \"F-test computed via direct numpy OLS (not statsmodels VAR) for computational efficiency. Granger F-stat: y[t] ~ c + y[t-1] + x[t-1] vs y[t] ~ c + y[t-1]. Permutation: 500 random shuffles of x, p = (count + 1)/(n_perms + 1).\"\n}\n",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Critical disk analysis:
- scale_69b_bf16: 13GB for 3 gens → ~4.3GB/gen → 10 gens total ≈ 43GB
- Currently 30GB free
- Remaining 7 gens ≈ 30GB more
- That's exactly at the limit, but a048 and a070 will also consume space (each ~2GB for 10 gens at 410m fp32)
- a010_bf16 is 1.6GB for 8 gens, so will add only ~0.4GB more — negligible


- The old fp32 scale_69b run barely ran (only 14MB), so that's not a concern. But the bf16 run will consume the remaining 30GB of free space, leaving nothing for a048 and a070, which each need about 2GB.

I need to check what old experiment data in scale_check/ can be deleted to free up space before the bf16 run fills the disk mid-experiment. I'm also trying to understand the checkpoint sizes — 4.3GB per generation for a 6.9B bf16 model seems reasonable if each checkpoint is around 13-14GB, though the exact breakdown depends on whether the script saves full checkpoints or just metrics per generation.
```

Looking at the other experiments on this server, a048 and a070 each need about 2GB for their 410m models, plus a010_bf16 at 1.6GB. That's roughly 4GB more on top of the 26GB peak for scale_69b, which leaves almost no buffer. The key is whether the cleanup script deletes old checkpoints before or immediately as new ones save—if three checkpoints ever coexist, I'll run out of space. The previous s42 run managed fine with just two generations, so this should be manageable if the timing works out. Looking at the other experiments running in parallel—a010_bf16 wrapping up around 2GB, a048 and a070 just starting, plus t20_a025 at 1.6GB and gpt2m still to check—the real constraint is whether we have enough headroom at the gen_2→gen_3 transition. Rather than overanalyze, I should flag this as a manageable risk if checkpoint rotation works, but recommend the Director preemptively free up the 52GB from old scale_check runs to be safe.
```

```text
Let me review the recent completed experiments. The most recent ones (by updated_at) are:

1. **alpha_sweep_a065_s42_fp32** (2026-05-21T01:30) — already reviewed
2. **alpha_sweep_a040_s42_fp32_v2** (2026-05-21T00:38) — needs review check
3. **interv_react_s43** (2026-05-21T00:36) — already reviewed
4. **scale_69b_a100_s43_fp32** (2026-05-20T23:24) — failed, already noted


5. **a040_v2** shows some striking patterns: PermG completely failed at α=0.40 with zero detections, but K3_3sigma jumped dramatically from 1/8 to 6/8 when applying the D111 direction filter, suggesting that filter was crucial for this configuration. The threshold_onset method performed perfectly at 8/8, while naive_xcorr achieved 4/8 post-filter, and I'm noticing the drift metrics show meaningful degradation—entropy dropped 19% and distinct token count fell 35%.

The disconnect between PermG's zero detections and threshold_onset's perfect score makes sense: PermG is looking for temporal lead-lag relationships (Granger causality), while threshold_onset just checks whether both signals cross 3σ thresholds regardless of causal ordering. At α=0.40, we're below the critical point around 0.48, so PermG's failure aligns with expectations from the sigmoid fit. Looking at the dose response curve, α=0.25 showed variable PermG results depending on seed, and now at α=0.40 it's completely absent—this suggests we're solidly in the regime where Granger causality breaks down. I'm checking whether a040_v2 is a fresh run or a re-run of the original a040, and I notice the direction filter was applied correctly this time—the D111_direction_check field shows the expected metric changes from the filtering. The metrics appear internally consistent, so I should verify what's actually different between the two versions before deciding if I need a fresh review.

At α=0.40, the PermG=0/8 confirms we're below the critical threshold, and while threshold_onset shows 8/8 detections, K3 only catches 1/8—indicating individual methods are picking up something but there's no reliable consensus at this α. Since this looks like it was already reviewed, I should move on to checking the running experiments and verifying whether the a065 registry entry still has those old field naming issues.

So a065 is still using non-standard field names — `ty_granger_sig` instead of `ty_forward_sig`, and `robust_k3_sig` instead of `robust_pairs_K3`. Even though it switched to `permg_fwd_sig` which is more descriptive, the inconsistency with the canonical format persists.

Looking at the full list of deviations in a065, there are multiple fields that don't match the standard naming convention, including some that have no equivalent in the standard schema at all. This is a naming regression, though the underlying data appears correct — it's just non-standard. I've already flagged this issue, so let me continue checking what else needs attention.

The a065 analysis review was already posted to the Director with a warning about the naming regression, so re-reporting it wouldn't be productive. Instead, I should focus on tracking any new experiment completions or emerging issues.

The α=0.48 experiment is particularly important since it's at the phase transition critical point identified from the sigmoid fit. If PermG shows a positive value at this alpha, it confirms the sigmoid transition model; if it's zero, that sharpens our understanding of where the critical point actually falls. The α=0.70 data point will fill in another region of the parameter space.

The 5method_summary uses the D102 analysis script which might compute threshold_onset differently than the D105 3σ method.

Looking at the D111_direction_check filtering in the registry, it doesn't show threshold_onset being filtered at all, which is odd if the pre-filter value was 0/8. Looking at the 5method_leadlag.json file, it confirms the correct D105 3σ method was used and returned 0/8, so the registry value of 8/8 appears to be incorrect — likely a leftover from an older analysis or a copy-paste error when the registry was populated. But threshold_onset doesn't appear in that filter list, yet the registry shows it at 8/8 while the method summary shows 0/8, which suggests they're measuring different things—one using the D105 true 3σ threshold versus something else. of 8 downstream metrics crossed the 3σ threshold.

But the registry's threshold_onset_3sigma=8/8 is claiming all 8 crossed! This is clearly wrong.

This looks like a genuine data error in the registry. Let me report it.

Actually, wait. Let me reconsider. The threshold_onset analysis checks: does the downstream metric cross a 3σ threshold at any point? For α=0.40 where drift_distinct1=-35.48%, drift_distinct2=-48.01%, drift_distinct3=-48.42%, there IS substantial downstream drift. The 3σ question is whether this drift exceeds three standard deviations of the noise. In the 5method_summary, threshold_onset=0/8 uses "true_3sigma_d105" which computes the threshold from the standard deviation of the full time series. With α=0.40 showing moderate but smooth decline, the std of the full series might be large enough that the drift doesn't exceed 3σ. But the registry claims 8/8 — every pair crosses threshold. This could be from an older analysis that used a lower threshold or a different std computation. Either way, the server's 5method analysis is authoritative and says 0/8, so the registry value of 8/8 is wrong. Let me report this.

Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

1	# T=20 Power Theory (√T Scaling) Verification Report
2	
3	**Date**: 2026-05-21

7	
8	---
9	
10	## 1. Theory Summary
11	
12	**Power Theory** posits that PermG detection rate scales with time-series length T via a √T effect-size boost:
13	
14	$$z_{T=20} = z_{T=10} \times \sqrt{20/10} = z_{T=10} \times 1.414$$
15	
16	$$\text{rate}_{T=20} = \Phi(z_{T=20})$$
17	
18	A sigmoid was fit to T=10 PermG rates across α (excluding α=0.60 sync dip), then extrapolated to T=20 via probit-domain √T scaling. Bootstrap N=10,000 (seed=42) quantified parameter uncertainty.
19	
20	**T=10 sigmoid parameters**: k=200.0, α_c=0.4755, y_max=0.7518  
21	**Bootstrap 95% CI**: α_c ∈ [0.425, 0.558], y_max ∈ [0.691, 0.895]
22	
23	---
24	
25	## 2. Prediction vs. Observation
26	
27	| α | T=10 PermG | Predicted T=20 (point) | Predicted range | Rate 95% CI | **Observed T=20** | **Verdict** |
28	|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|:---:|
29	| 0.25 | 0/8 | — | — | — | RUNNING | — |
30	| 0.45 | 0/8 | 0/8 | [0, 7] | [0.015, 0.828] | **5/8** | **FAIL** (point) |
31	| 0.50 | 6/8 | 7/8 | [0, 7] | [0.015, 0.830] | **6/8** | **PASS** |
32	| 0.60 | 0/8 | 0/8 | [0, 0] | [0.015, 0.015] | **0/8** | **PASS** |

35	
36	**Overall verification rate: 4/5 PASS (80%)**
37

45

47	|--------|------|------|---|
48	| PermG fwd | 0/8 | 5/8 | +5 |
49	| PermG rev | 0/8 | 0/8 | 0 |
50	| Diff XCorr | 3/8 | 7/8 | +4 |

54	**Point prediction failed** (0/8 → 5/8), but observed rate 0.625 falls within the bootstrap 95% CI [0.015, 0.828]. The wide CI correctly reflected α_c uncertainty spanning the phase transition.
55	
56	**Scientific significance**: The blind zone at α=0.45 is a **statistical power problem, not a fundamental limit**. Doubling T recovers strong PermG detection (0→5), confirming that α_c(T) decreases with T. At T=10, α_c ≈ 0.475; at T=20, α_c < 0.45. The sigmoid's steepness near α_c amplifies small boundary shifts into large rate changes, making point predictions fragile but the qualitative direction (increased detection) correct.

Mauve's near-flat trajectory across generations makes it structurally resistant to PermG detection regardless of T. This 6/8 ceiling is not a power limitation but a **metric property**: mauve measures distributional similarity in a way that is insensitive to the cumulative contamination process.

116	
117	All 5 point predictions were 0/8 or 7/8 (sigmoid extremes). All 5 observed values were 0/8, 5/8, or 6/8.
